The December 6, 2019 Decision and the October 23, 2020 Resolution of the Court of Appeals in CA-G.R. CV No. 112231 are REVERSED and SET ASIDE. The August 17, 2017 Decision and the February 22, 2018 Resolution of Branch 109, Regional Trial Court, Pasay City in Civil Case No. R-PSY-16-21858-CV declaring the marriage between petitioner Marie Anne Grace Chua-Mascariñas and respondent Martin S. Mascariñas, Jr. null and void under Article 36 of the Family Code are REINSTATED.<br><br>SO ORDERED.[^677] |
| *Alfonso v. Alfonso* | ACCORDINGLY, the Petition for Review on Certiorari is GRANTED. The November 26, 2020 Decision and the June 23, 2021 Resolution of the Court of Appeals in CA-G.R. CV No. 113342 are REVERSED and SET ASIDE. The marriage of petitioner Arnold S.I. Alfonso and private respondent Michelle Pamintuan Alfonso is DECLARED VOID on the ground of psychological incapacity.<br><br>SO ORDERED.[^678] |
| [*Tee Ten v. Tee Ten*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70118) | ACCORDINGLY, the Petition is DENIED for lack of merit. The Decision dated May 4, 2021, and the Resolution dated December 13, 2021, of the Court of Appeals in CA-G.R. CV No. 109458 are AFFIRMED.<br><br>The case is hereby REMANDED to Branch 261, Regional Trial Court, Pasig City, for the conduct of further proceedings in relation to: (1) the determination of the proper amount of support pendente lite, if still warranted under the circumstances; and (2) the liquidation, partition, and distribution of the co-owned properties of Willy Q. Tee Ten and Nellie Y. Chan Tee Ten in accordance with Article 147 of the Family Code.<br><br>SO ORDERED.[^679] |
| [*Candano-Lim v. Lim*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/69825) | WHEREFORE, the Petition for Certiorari is DENIED for lack of merit. The Decision dated October 18, 2021, and Resolution dated August 1, 2022, of the Court of Appeals in CA-G.R. SP No. 159191 and CA-G.R. SP No. 167052 are AFFIRMED.<br><br>SO ORDERED.[^680] |
| [*Republic v. Tangarorang*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/69852) | ACCORDINGLY, the Petition for Review on Certiorari is GRANTED. The August 29, 2023 Decision of Branch 1, Regional Trial Court, Butuan City in Civil Case No. 2021-0141 is AFFIRMED with MODIFICATION. Sharemahlyne Librada Tangarorang is DECLARED a legitimate child of respondents Linney Jean L. Tangarorang and Ramer R. Tangarorang, pursuant to Article 54 of the Family Code.<br><br>Further, the February 7, 2024 Order of the Regional Trial Court is REVERSED and SET ASIDE.<br><br>SO ORDERED.[^681] |
| [*Maliwat-Melad v. Melad*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/69969) | ACCORDINGLY, the petition is DISMISSED. The October 27, 2022 Decision and the May 17, 2023 Resolution of the Court of Appeals in CA-G.R. CV No. 117590, upholding the March 1, 2021 Decision of Branch 11, Family Court, Tarlac City is AFFIRMED.<br><br>SO ORDERED.[^682] |
* ***Kusk v. Kusk* (August 13, 2025):** Penned by Senior Associate Justice Marvic M.V.F. Leonen, the Court reversed the Court of Appeals and declared void the marriage between Filipina petitioner Milagrosa Villarey Kusk and Danish national Torben Kusk.[^683]  Applying [*Tan-Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507), the Court held that the totality of evidence (including the testimony of Milagrosa and the psychological evaluation of clinical psychologist Dr. Nedy Tayag, who evaluated Milagrosa while Torben did not participate) established that both spouses suffered from grave, antecedent, and legally incurable personality structures (Milagrosa with Narcissistic Personality Disorder and Torben with Passive-Aggressive Personality Disorder manifesting in chronic alcoholism, physical violence, financial irresponsibility, and permanent desertion for over a decade), and reiterated that personal examination of the absentee foreign respondent was unnecessary.[^684] 
* ***Laguda v. Manguardia-Laguda* (January 29, 2026):** Denied a husband's petition for declaration of nullity anchored on both an allegedly false affidavit of five-year cohabitation under Article 34 and the wife's alleged psychological incapacity under Article 36; held that the petitioner failed to overcome the presumption of regularity of the notarized joint affidavit of cohabitation and failed to prove psychological incapacity by clear and convincing evidence where his lone corroborating witness met the wife only after the wedding and the allegations showed only ordinary marital friction over finances and communication.[^685] 
* ***Republic v. Ramoran-Wong* (February 3, 2026):** Denied the OSG's petition for review and affirmed the Court of Appeals' declaration of absolute nullity of the marriage between Theresa Ramoran-Wong and Vincent Wong.[^686]  Applying [*Tan-Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507), the Court held that Theresa proved juridical antecedence, gravity, and legal incurability by clear and convincing evidence through the testimonies of Vincent's own father and cousin (who witnessed his formative childhood marked by early parental separation, an irresponsible and unfaithful father, and pre-marital behavioral deviance) alongside Dr. Alhama's psychological evaluation of both spouses diagnosing Vincent with Mixed Personality Disorder with Histrionic, Narcissistic and Antisocial traits.[^687] 
* ***Soto v. Reyes-Soto* (April 22, 2026):** Landmark 2026 decision applying [*Tan-Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507) and [*Diño*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/33909) to deny the husband's appeal and affirm the Court of Appeals' and Regional Trial Court's declaration of nullity of the marriage between Juanito B. Soto and Cassandra Reyes-Soto.[^688]  In [*Soto*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70372), psychiatrist Dr. Villegas personally interviewed both spouses and established that both harbored deeply rooted, mutually antagonistic personality structures antedating the wedding, with Reyes-Soto having developed a Passive-Aggressive Personality Structure under a strict, disciplinarian, and emotionally detached mother, and Soto having developed an Inadequate Personality Structure incapable of emotional intimacy or autonomous decision-making apart from his parents.[^689]  The Court held that their opposing personality structures trapped the union in a prolonged state of emotional limbo and inevitable breakdown, satisfying gravity, juridical antecedence, and legal incurability by clear and convincing evidence, and further reiterated that the partition of co-owned properties under Article 147 is not a prerequisite to the declaration of nullity of marriage under Article 36.[^690]

---

## Related Provisions

- **[Article 1](art-001.md)** to **[Article 7](art-007.md)** define marriage as a special contract of permanent union between a man and a woman for the establishment of conjugal and family life, and prescribe the essential and formal requisites of marriage.
- **[Article 26](art-026.md)** governs the validity of foreign marriages and the judicial recognition of a foreign divorce obtained by either the alien or Filipino spouse in a mixed marriage, providing an independent statutory remedy distinct from a declaration of nullity under Article 36. [^691] 
- **[Article 35](art-035.md)**, **[Article 37](art-037.md)**, and **[Article 38](art-038.md)** enumerate marriages that are void *ab initio* for absence of essential or formal requisites, incest, or public policy, which differ from Article 36 in that their nullity rests on objective statutory impediments rather than a party's personality structure. [^692] 
- **[Article 39](art-039.md)** (as amended by Republic Act No. 8533) declares that the action or defense for the declaration of absolute nullity of a marriage, including one anchored on psychological incapacity under Article 36, does not prescribe.
- **[Article 40](art-040.md)** provides that the absolute nullity of a previous marriage may be invoked for purposes of remarriage on the basis solely of a final judgment declaring such previous marriage void, while under [*Pulido v. People*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/67720) (2021), a judicial declaration of nullity under Article 36 retroacts to the date of celebration to negate criminal liability for bigamy. [^693] 
- **[Article 45](art-045.md)** and **[Article 46](art-046.md)** govern voidable marriages (including unsound mind, fraud, force, intimidation, and physical impotence), which are valid until annulled and subject to ratification by free cohabitation, unlike an Article 36 marriage which is void *ab initio* and incapable of ratification. [^694] 
- **[Article 47](art-047.md)**, **[Article 48](art-048.md)**, and **[Article 49](art-049.md)** mandate the appearance of the prosecuting attorney or Solicitor General on behalf of the State to prevent collusion and fabrication of evidence, and govern support and custody *pendente lite*.
- **[Article 50](art-050.md)**, **[Article 51](art-051.md)**, **[Article 52](art-052.md)**, **[Article 53](art-053.md)**, and **[Article 54](art-054.md)** govern the effects of final judgments of nullity, registration of the decree in the civil registry, and expressly classify children conceived or born before the finality of a judgment of nullity under Article 36 as **legitimate**; under [*Valdes v. Regional Trial Court*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/33980) (1996) and [*Diño v. Diño*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/33909) (2011), however, the liquidation and delivery-of-presumptive-legitime rules of Articles 50–52 do not apply to Article 36 marriages. [^695] [^696] 
- **[Article 55](../title-02-legal-separation/art-055.md)** enumerates the grounds for legal separation (such as repeated physical violence, drug addiction, alcoholism, lesbianism or homosexuality, sexual infidelity, and abandonment), which arise after the marriage and do not sever the marital bond unless shown to be manifestations of an antecedent, grave, and legally incurable personality structure under Article 36. [^697] [^698] 
- **[Article 68](../title-03-rights-and-obligations-between-husband-a/art-068.md)** to **[Article 71](../title-03-rights-and-obligations-between-husband-a/art-071.md)** define the essential marital obligations between husband and wife, including the duties to live together, observe mutual love, respect, and fidelity, render mutual help and support, fix the family domicile, and jointly support and manage the household; non-compliance with these obligations due to an antecedent personality structure triggers Article 36. [^699] 
- **[Article 147](../title-04-property-relations-between-husband-and-w/art-147.md)** and **[Article 148](../title-04-property-relations-between-husband-and-w/art-148.md)** govern the special co-ownership property regime of unions declared void *ab initio* under Article 36. [^700] [^701] 
- **[Article 213](../title-09-parental-authority/art-213.md)**, **[Article 220](../title-09-parental-authority/art-220.md)**, and **[Article 221](../title-09-parental-authority/art-221.md)** define parental authority and the tender-age presumption (under which no child under seven years of age shall be separated from the mother unless the court finds compelling reasons), as well as the essential parental obligations toward children. [^702] [^703] 

## Key Cases

- **[*Soto v. Reyes-Soto*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70372)** (G.R. No. 249759, April 22, 2026): Affirmed the declaration of nullity of marriage under Article 36 where both spouses harbored deeply rooted, mutually antagonistic personality structures antedating the wedding (Passive-Aggressive Personality Structure in the wife and Inadequate Personality Structure in the husband) that trapped their union in prolonged emotional limbo and inevitable breakdown, and reiterated that partition of co-owned properties under Article 147 is not a prerequisite to the declaration of nullity.[^704] 
- **[*Republic v. Ramoran-Wong*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70390)** (G.R. No. 276986, February 3, 2026): Affirmed the declaration of nullity of marriage under [*Tan-Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507) where the husband's Mixed Personality Disorder with Histrionic, Narcissistic and Antisocial traits and juridical antecedence were established by clear and convincing evidence through the testimonies of his own father and cousin regarding his formative childhood environment alongside expert psychological evaluation.[^705] 
- **[*Laguda v. Manguardia-Laguda*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70349)** (G.R. No. 279718, January 29, 2026): Denied a husband's petition for declaration of nullity under Articles 34 and 36 where his lone corroborating witness met the respondent wife only after the wedding and the allegations depicted only ordinary marital friction over finances and communication rather than an antecedent dysfunctional personality structure.[^706] 
- **[*Kusk v. Kusk*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70120)** (G.R. No. 228564, August 13, 2025): Reversed the Court of Appeals and declared void the marriage between a Filipina petitioner and a Danish national where lay and expert evidence established mutually incompatible Narcissistic and Passive-Aggressive personality structures manifesting in chronic alcoholism, physical abuse, financial irresponsibility, and permanent desertion, confirming that personal examination of an absentee foreign spouse is unnecessary.[^707] 
- **[*Boado v. Galvez-Boado*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/69865)** (G.R. No. 263627, November 4, 2024): The marriage was declared void due to husband Ronald's incapacity. The decision identifies his strict upbringing and inability to meet emotional obligations; the diagnosis recorded was Passive-Aggressive Personality Disorder.[^708] [^709]
- **[*Quiogue, Jr. v. Quiogue*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68704)** (G.R. No. 203992, August 22, 2022): "Infidelity is not measured in terms of frequency."[^710] The Supreme Court required gravity, antecedence and legal incurability, and declared the marriage void because of Antonio's incapacity.[^711]
- **[*Alberto v. Alberto*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68353)** (G.R. No. 236827, April 19, 2022): Reinstated the trial court's declaration of nullity under [*Tan-Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507) where the husband's compulsive gambling, habitual lying, and complete abdication of spousal and parental responsibilities were traced by collateral informants and expert testimony to an overindulged, undisciplined childhood.[^712] 
- **[*Pulido v. People*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/67720)** (G.R. No. 220149, July 27, 2021, En Banc): "in criminal prosecutions for bigamy, the accused can validly interpose the defense of a void ab initio marriage even without obtaining a judicial declaration of absolute nullity."[^713] The decision separately rejects reliance on a second marriage that is void merely because it is bigamous.[^714]
- **[*Tan-Andal v. Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507)** (G.R. No. 196359, May 11, 2021, *En Banc*): Landmark decision that abandoned the medicalized *Molina* framework, set the quantum of proof at clear and convincing evidence, reconceptualized psychological incapacity as an enduring personality structure rather than a mental illness, dispensed with mandatory expert testimony and personal examination, redefined incurability in the legal and relational sense, and applied Article 147 co-ownership to property acquired during the void union.[^715] 
- **[*Kalaw v. Fernandez*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/59147)** (G.R. No. 166357, January 14, 2015): Reversed its earlier 2011 ruling on reconsideration and declared the marriage void where the wife's compulsive mahjong gambling and habitual neglect of her young children demonstrated a grave, antecedent inability to fulfill maternal and conjugal obligations, cautioning that *Molina* must not be applied as an unbending straitjacket.[^716] 
- **[*Diño v. Diño*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/33909)** (G.R. No. 178044, January 19, 2011): Held that Article 50 of the Family Code and Section 19(1) of A.M. No. 02-11-10-SC requiring prior liquidation, partition, and delivery of presumptive legitimes before issuance of the Decree of Absolute Nullity apply only to Article 40 and Article 45 marriages, and do not apply to Article 36 marriages governed by Article 147.[^717] 
- **[*Ngo Te v. Yu-Te*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/48851)** (G.R. No. 161793, February 13, 2009): Critiqued how the *Molina* guidelines had functioned as a rigid straitjacket contrary to the Family Code Revision Committee's intent and declared void a marriage involving Antisocial and Dependent Personality Disorders.[^718] 
- **[*Antonio v. Reyes*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/40916)** (G.R. No. 155800, March 10, 2006): Declared void a marriage where the respondent wife's persistent, pathological lying and fabrication of an imaginary existence rendered her unable to comply with the essential obligations of mutual love, respect, and trust under Article 68.[^719] 
- **[*Buenaventura v. Court of Appeals*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/42809)** (G.R. No. 127358, March 31, 2005): Held that moral and exemplary damages and attorney's fees cannot be awarded against a spouse solely by reason of a declaration of psychological incapacity under Article 36, because psychological incapacity is an involuntary condition incompatible with willful malice or bad faith.[^720] 
- **[*Marcos v. Marcos*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/38116)** (G.R. No. 136490, October 19, 2000): Held that personal medical or psychological examination of the respondent spouse is not a *conditio sine qua non* for a declaration of nullity under Article 36 if the totality of evidence suffices.[^721] 
- **[*Republic v. Court of Appeals and Molina*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/34463)** (G.R. No. 108763, February 13, 1997, *En Banc*): Promulgated the eight guidelines for Article 36 petitions, which governed for twenty-four years until their clinical and expert-witness mandates were abandoned or modified in [*Tan-Andal v. Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507) (2021).[^722] 
- **[*Chi Ming Tsoi v. Court of Appeals*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/34416)** (G.R. No. 119190, January 16, 1997): First Supreme Court decision affirming a declaration of nullity under Article 36, holding that a physically normal husband's senseless, protracted refusal to consummate the marriage through sexual intercourse throughout ten months of cohabitation manifested grave psychological incapacity.[^723] 
- **[*Valdes v. Regional Trial Court, Branch 102, Quezon City*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/33980)** (G.R. No. 122749, July 31, 1996): Established that property relations in a marriage declared void under Article 36 are governed by the special co-ownership rules of Article 147 (or Article 148) of the Family Code rather than Absolute Community or Conjugal Partnership under Articles 50–52.[^724] 
- **[*Santos v. Court of Appeals*](https://lawphil.net/judjuris/juri1995/jan1995/gr_112019_1995.html)** (G.R. No. 112019, January 4, 1995, *En Banc*): Foundational decision establishing that psychological incapacity under Article 36 must be characterized by (a) gravity, (b) juridical antecedence, and (c) incurability.[^725] 

## Suggested Answers

**Q1: Hypothetical: A spouse presents a psychologist who did not personally interview the absent respondent. Assume: the totality of the evidence clearly and convincingly establishes gravity, juridical antecedence and legal incurability; must the petition fail solely because the respondent was not examined?**
No. In [*Kusk v. Kusk*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/70120), the Supreme Court stated: "the psychological or medical examination of a respondent spouse is not required as a condition for the declaration of nullity of marriage, as psychological incapacity is to be determined through all the evidence presented."[^726] On the assumed evidence, the absence of that interview does not by itself defeat the petition.[^727]

**Q2: Hypothetical: Assume: a final judgment declaring A's first marriage void under Article 36 is obtained only after A's second marriage. May A offer that judgment as a defense in the bigamy case?**
Yes. In [*Pulido v. People*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/67720), the Supreme Court stated: "Consequently, a judicial declaration of absolute nullity of the first and/or subsequent marriages obtained by the accused in a separate proceeding, irrespective of the time within which they are secured, is a valid defense in the criminal prosecution for bigamy."[^728] The later date alone does not bar A from relying on the assumed judgment declaring the first marriage void.[^729]

**Q3: Hypothetical: Assume: two parties were capacitated to marry, lived exclusively together under an Article 36 marriage and acquired a home from one party's salary while the other cared for their family and household. Does the absence of a cash contribution alone exclude the homemaker from co-ownership?**
No. In [*Tan-Andal v. Andal*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/68507), the Supreme Court reproduced Article 147: "For purposes of this Article, a party who did not participate in the acquisition by the other party of any property shall be deemed to have contributed jointly in the acquisition thereof if the former's efforts consisted in the care and maintenance of the family and of the household."[^730] The assumed household contribution must therefore be considered, unlike Mario's proven lack of contribution to the specific lot and duplex in that case.[^731]

**Q4: Hypothetical: A child was born before her parents' wedding. Assume: the parents were not disqualified by an impediment to marry at conception and their marriage was later declared void under Article 36; does the nullity judgment make their legitimated child illegitimate?**
No. In [*Republic v. Tangarorang*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/69852), the Supreme Court stated: "However, for void marriages under Article 36, Article 54 of the Family Code did not distinguish between a child born prior to the marriage and a child born during the subsisting marriage."[^732] On those facts the child retains legitimate status despite the Article 36 judgment.[^733]

**Q5: Hypothetical: A husband provided financial support for years but later established an inability to fulfill other essential marital obligations rooted in his upbringing. Assume: gravity, juridical antecedence and legal incurability are proved; does his earlier performance of some duties automatically defeat the petition?**
No. In [*Boado v. Galvez-Boado*](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/1/69865), the Supreme Court stated: "Besides, just because a spouse was once able to perform some marital obligations does not mean that they cannot be subsequently incapable of fulfilling some of the other obligations."[^734] The Supreme Court declared the Boado marriage void due to husband Ronald's own psychological incapacity.[^735]

# Quotes

> "It should be obvious, looking at all the foregoing disquisitions, including, and most importantly, the deliberations of the Family Code Revision Committee itself, that the use of the phrase "psychological incapacity" under Article 36 of the Code has not been meant to comprehend all such possible cases of psychoses as, likewise mentioned by some ecclesiastical authorities, extremely low intelligence, immaturity, and like circumstances (cited in Fr. Artemio Baluma's "Void and Voidable Marriages in the Family Code and their Parallels in Canon Law," quoting from the Diagnostic Statistical Manual of Mental Disorder by the American Psychiatric Association; Edward Hudson's "Handbook II for Marriage Nullity Cases"). Article 36 of the Family Code cannot be taken and construed independently of, but must stand in conjunction with, existing precepts in our law on marriage. Thus correlated, "psychological incapacity" should refer to no less than a mental (not physical) incapacity that causes a party to be truly incognitive of the basic marital covenants that concomitantly must be assumed and discharged by the parties to the marriage which, as so expressed by Article 68 of the Family Code, include their mutual obligations to live together, observe love, respect and fidelity and render help and support. There is hardly any doubt that the intendment of the law has been to confine the meaning of "psychological incapacity" to the most serious cases of personality disorders clearly demonstrative of an utter intensitivity or inability to give meaning and significance to the marriage. This pschologic condition must exist at the time the marriage is celebrated. The law does not evidently envision, upon the other hand, an inability of the spouse to have sexual relations with the other. This conclusion is implicit under Article 54 of the Family Code which considers children conceived prior to the judicial declaration of nullity of the void marriage to be "legitimate.""[^736]

> "The trial court correctly applied the law. In a void marriage, regardless of the cause thereof, the property relations of the parties during the period of cohabitation is governed by the provisions of Article 147 or Article 148, such as the case may be, of the Family Code. Article 147 is a remake of Article 144 of the Civil Code as interpreted and so applied in previous cases; it provides:"[^737]

> "While the law provides that the husband and the wife are obliged to live together, observe mutual love, respect and fidelity (Art. 68, Family Code), the sanction therefor is actually the "spontaneous, mutual affection between husband and wife and not any legal mandate or court order" (Cuaderno vs. Cuaderno, 120 Phil. 1298). Love is useless unless it is shared with another. Indeed, no man is an island, the cruelest act of a partner in marriage is to say "I could not have cared less." This is so because an ungiven self is an unfulfilled self. The egoist has nothing but himself. In the natural order, it is sexual intimacy which brings spouses wholeness and oneness. Sexual intimacy is a gift and a participation in the mystery of creation. It is a function which enlivens the hope of procreation and ensures the continuation of family relations."[^738]

> "From their submissions and the Court’s own deliberations, the following guidelines in the interpretation and application of Art. 36 of the Family Code are hereby handed down for the guidance of the bench and the bar:"[^739]

> "Article 36 of the Family Code, we stress, is not to be confused with a divorce law that cuts the marital bond at the time the causes therefor manifest themselves. It refers to a serious psychological illness afflicting a party even before the celebration of the marriage. It is a malady so grave and so permanent as to deprive one of awareness of the duties and responsibilities of the matrimonial bond one is about to assume. These marital obligations are those provided under Articles 68 to 71, 220, 221 and 225 of the Family Code."[^740]

> "For the same reason, since psychological incapacity means that one is truly incognitive of the basic marital covenants that one must assume and discharge as a consequence of marriage, it removes the basis for the contention that the petitioner purposely deceived the private respondent. If the private respondent was deceived, it was not due to a willful act on the part of the petitioner. Therefore, the award of moral damages was without basis in law and in fact."[^741]

> "But the Constitution itself does not establish the parameters of state protection to marriage as a social institution and the foundation of the family. It remains the province of the legislature to define all legal aspects of marriage and prescribe the strategy and the modalities to protect it, based on whatever socio-political influences it deems proper, and subject of course to the qualification that such legislative enactment itself adheres to the Constitution and the Bill of Rights. This being the case, it also falls on the legislature to put into operation the constitutional provisions that protect marriage and the family. This has been accomplished at present through the enactment of the Family Code, which defines marriage and the family, spells out the corresponding legal effects, imposes the limitations that affect married and family life, as well as prescribes the grounds for declaration of nullity and those for legal separation. While it may appear that the judicial denial of a petition for declaration of nullity is reflective of the constitutional mandate to protect marriage, such action in fact merely enforces a statutory definition of marriage, not a constitutionally ordained decree of what marriage is. Indeed, if circumstances warrant, Sections 1 and 2 of Article XV need not be the only constitutional considerations to be taken into account in resolving a petition for declaration of nullity."[^742]

> "In dissolving marital bonds on account of either party's psychological incapacity, the Court is not demolishing the foundation of families, but it is actually protecting the sanctity of marriage, because it refuses to allow a person afflicted with a psychological disorder, who cannot comply with or assume the essential marital obligations, from remaining in that sacred bond. It may be stressed that the infliction of physical violence, constitutional indolence or laziness, drug dependence or addiction, and psychosexual anomaly are manifestations of a sociopathic personality anomaly. Let it be noted that in Article 36, there is no marriage to speak of in the first place, as the same is void from the very beginning. To indulge in imagery, the declaration of nullity under Article 36 will simply provide a decent burial to a stillborn marriage."[^743]

> "It is clear from Article 50 of the Family Code that Section 19(1) of the Rule applies only to marriages which are declared void ab initio or annulled by final judgment under Articles 40 and 45 of the Family Code. In short, Article 50 of the Family Code does not apply to marriages which are declared void ab initio under Article 36 of the Family Code, which should be declared void without waiting for the liquidation of the properties of the parties."[^744]

> "The foregoing guidelines have turned out to be rigid, such that their application to every instance practically condemned the petitions for declaration of nullity to the fate of certain rejection. But Article 36 of the Family Code must not be so strictly and too literally read and applied given the clear intendment of the drafters to adopt its enacted version of “less specificity” obviously to enable “some resiliency in its application.” Instead, every court should approach the issue of nullity “not on the basis of a priori assumptions, predilections or generalizations, but according to its own facts” in recognition of the verity that no case would be on “all fours” with the next one in the field of psychological incapacity as a ground for the nullity of marriage; hence, every “trial judge must take pains in examining the factual milieu and the appellate court must, as much as possible, avoid substituting its own judgment for that of the trial court.”"[^745]

> "In light of the foregoing, this Court now categorically abandons the second Molina guideline. Psychological incapacity is neither a mental incapacity nor a personality disorder that must be proven through expert opinion. There must be proof, however, of the durable or enduring aspects of a person's personality, called "personality structure," which manifests itself through clear acts of dysfunctionality that undermines the family. The spouse's personality structure must make it impossible for him or her to understand and, more important, to comply with his or her essential marital obligations."[^746]

> "Furthermore, not being an illness in a medical sense, psychological incapacity is not something to be cured. And even if it were a mental disorder, it cannot be described in terms of being curable or incurable. Dean Estrada-Claudio explained that true mental disorders follow a probable course or outcome, called "prognosis," that can either be self-limited or remain "stable across time and consistent in situations." If self-limited, the disorder is, in layperson's terms, "curable." If it has poor long-term prognosis, the disorder is said to be "incurable.""[^747]

> "To summarize and for future guidance, the parties are not required to obtain a judicial declaration of absolute nullity of a void ab initio first and subsequent marriages in order to raise it as a defense in a bigamy case. The same rule now applies to all marriages celebrated under the Civil Code and the Family Code. Article 40 of the Family Code did not amend Article 349 of the RPC, and thus, did not deny the accused the right to collaterally attack the validity of a void ab initio marriage in the criminal prosecution for bigamy."[^748]

> "Through the landmark case of Tan-Andal v. Andal (Tan-Andal), the Court has clarified that psychological incapacity is neither a mental incapacity nor a personality disorder that must be proven through expert opinion. In addition, the Court declared:"[^749]

> "Under Article 68 of the Family Code, the "husband and wife are obliged to live together, observe mutual love, respect and fidelity, and render mutual help and support." Clearly, the law itself recognizes fidelity as the norm and a spouse should not be made to settle for anything less than absolute faithfulness from the other. This stems from the nature of marriage being a "special contract" of an exclusive partnership between a man and a woman. While it is true that infidelity is a ground for legal separation, the same may also be an indication of a psychological incapacity if, for the same reason, one is completely unable to discharge the essential obligations of marriage."[^750]

> "At this juncture, it is significant to note that the psychological or medical examination of a respondent spouse is not required as a condition for the declaration of nullity of marriage, as psychological incapacity is to be determined through all the evidence presented. Nonetheless, the psychological examination of Dr. Tayag must be given probative value as an expert's opinion."[^751]

> "The concept of juridical antecedence includes the ordinary experiences of the spouses not only prior to the marriage itself, but more importantly, during their "lived conjugal life." Theresa's testimony about her experiences with Vincent, both as a girlfriend and a wife, sufficiently demonstrates that his psychological incapacity was already present at the time of their marriage."[^752]

> "Viewed as a whole, the evidence reveals a marriage impaired at its very core by the parties' incompatible and mutually reinforcing psychological limitations. On the one hand, the petitioner entered the marriage emotionally dependent on his mother, unable to express, affection or reach outward with emotional assurance, and constrained by unresolved emotional conflicts that inhibited intimacy and partnership. His difficulty in separating filial attachment from marital commitment prevented him from becoming emotionally present to his wife and child. On the other hand, the respondent came into the marriage bearing the imprint of emotional deprivation in her formative years, longing for affection and affirmation, yet responding to disappointment not with engagement but with withdrawal, passivity, and unexpressed resentment. Her expectations of love remained unmet, while her manner of coping rendered her unable to bridge the growing emotional distance between them."[^753]

# Tips

> [!TIP]
> **Bar Exam Focus:** Keep four distinctions in view:
> 1. **Quantum and gravity (*Tan-Andal*):** "the plaintiff-spouse must prove his or her case with clear and convincing evidence."[^754] "In other words, it must be shown that the incapacity is caused by a genuinely serious psychic cause."[^755]
> 2. **Antecedence and marital experience (*Ramoran-Wong*):** "The concept of juridical antecedence includes the ordinary experiences of the spouses not only prior to the marriage itself, but more importantly, during their "lived conjugal life.""[^756]
> 3. **Expert evidence (*Kusk*):** "The absence of such personal examination is not fatal so long as the totality of evidence sufficiently supports a finding of psychological incapacity."[^757]
> 4. **The actual property finding (*Tan-Andal*):** "Mario has no share in this property because he did not care for and maintain the family and the household."[^758]

> [!NOTE]
> **Practical Application:**
> - **Check the whole record (*Kusk*):** Present the evidence of the claimed incapacity and assess expert opinion by its qualifications, method and factual basis; do not turn a suggested category of witnesses into an automatic requirement.[^759]
> - **Protect the child's established status (*Tangarorang*):** Account for the child's legitimation and Article 54 when framing the relief sought; birth before the wedding did not justify loss of legitimate status in that case.[^760]
> - **Distinguish the bigamy defense (*Pulido*):** Identify the asserted ground of nullity and its evidence; the Supreme Court expressly rejected the second marriage's being bigamous as a defense in itself.[^761]
